mr president, this report is another attempt to square the circle. it defends important things that we have pointed out, such as being against the nationalisation of the cap's costs, maintaining its budget, strengthening posei, defending small and mediumsized farmers. now, let us be serious, without calling into question the foundations of the cap, this is more of an exercise in hypocrisy, because it is this model of the cap, with its liberal orientation, that supports this model of intensive production geared to exports, to agribusiness, and it is this model that crushes farmers' prices and incomes. what we want is a break with this cap, with public instruments for regulating supply and allowing the defence of each country's food sovereignty and, in turn, guaranteeing fair and decent incomes to those who produce, our farmers.
mr president, the economic activity of the outermost regions is heavily dependent on agriculture. for this reason, the european parliament's position on the future of the cap, which we are voting on today, calls for the safeguarding of the posei budget and its regulatory provisions. the recognition that the outermost regions deserve differential treatment also with regard to the operation of the milk market should be emphasized, and the commission is asked to take into account indicators adapted to the reality of the outermost regions for monitoring milk prices and defining the crisis in the sector. in conclusion, i am in favour of greater external convergence of direct payments between member states in the first pillar of the cap, the logic of which should exclude the amounts they receive under the second pillar, and i voted in favour of this.
mr president, we already knew the general direction of this multiannual financial framework: less cohesion, less agricultural policy and more security and defence. the european commission confirmed this approach yesterday by announcing a 7% cut in structural funds for portugal and the maintenance of macroeconomic conditionality. we cannot accept that the application of the structural funds should be linked to the european semester and the country-specific recommendations, because this limits the capacity of the national states to decide on their own development model. this proposal from the european commission not only cuts into the structural funds but also creates new blackmail mechanisms to impose its neoliberal agenda on the national states. every day that passes it becomes absolutely clearer that neither this european commission nor this framework will be the ones to promote the social and territorial cohesion that they so much preach.
mr president, residence permits are temporary, at least in my country, and they can be a good thing or a bad thing. it depends on your control and what happens in one country is not necessarily replicable in all the others. in my case, my country is required to transfer capital of at least one million euros, create at least ten jobs and buy property worth at least 500,000 euros. since 2012, it has involved a revenue for portugal of eur 3,5 billion and investments of more than eur 84 million. obviously, a security check is required, a check on each application and, in the case of each conviction, expulsion and revocation of the benefit. but if the regime is enforced, it is not necessarily bad in the eyes of the law as long as the violator is ruthlessly condemned and tried.
mr president, security concerns dominate the management of the european union's borders, but only as regards migrants and refugees, the most vulnerable people in the world. for the rich and powerful, criminals and corrupt, several member states, including my own, have shamefully opened safe channels for access to residence and citizenship in the european union through gold visas and the direct sale of nationality. over the past five years, i have been warning national and european authorities that these schemes encourage the abuse of corrupt political officials and all kinds of intermediaries, as the trial in portugal of a minister and other senior state officials demonstrates. they are a threat to the organization, the integrity of the financial system and the security of our citizens, they attract kleptocrats, criminal organizations that seek refuge in the european union to launder money. as our inquiry into the panama papers demonstrated, due diligence about big applicants is perfunctory. banks even offer credits to obfuscate the origin of the money transfers involved. these schemes also exacerbate the gulf between the "haves" and the "have nots". transnational networks that market such schemes to russian, chinese and other rich people that they designate as global citizens, such as henley and partners, the arton group that hires mr. duran barroso for promotional events, the big 4, and so on, need to be scrutinized in their activities, profits, processes and contracts binding eu governments, that even compel prime ministers to go on roadshows selling european citizenship, as does the prime minister of malta. this race to the bottom has to end. the commission and the council must act.
mr president, i would like to say that in this debate we must distinguish clearly between what constitutes a residence permit and what constitutes the grant of nationality. it is also essential that the residence permit itself be subject to strict criteria, particularly in terms of criminal records and, of course, the background of the applicants. i have done that, and i have also informed the european union. but we cannot go on from there to the conclusion that everything that is the granting of a residence permit and even, in certain cases, of nationality, subject to the common requirements and assumptions, is something that cannot be done by the states. it is interesting to note that many states that are doing nothing to take in refugees are here invoking refugees to oppose these programs. this is the case in the netherlands, mrs int veld, where 20 of the largest portuguese companies are registered, and then the portuguese state does not want to have programmes to attract investment, when all the investment is going to the netherlands, which is preaching austerity to us. if there were also tax harmonization, perhaps there would be no need for this kind of differentiation.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the budget is properly managed. mr president, commissioner, first of all, congratulations on the commission's proposal which meets our expectations. as we all know, portugal experienced a tragedy in 2017 due to the fires that occurred. more than 100 lives were lost, a tragedy that must not be repeated. if this mechanism were in place, we could have saved these lives, and that's our obligation, to save human lives. i therefore welcome this proposal, this new mechanism, and i also want to tell you that i will see what solidarity the member states will show when we discuss budgetary matters. for 2019 and 2020, we need 280 million euros. new priorities also require new financial resources, and that is why parliament is calling for the additional eur 280 million to be earmarked for them and not for cuts in other policies. this is our defence and we also count on the support of the commission in this respect. this mechanism must not forget prevention and is obviously complementary to, and not replacing, the member states. i would like to end by congratulating the two rapporteurs on their work.
we are in favour of cooperation between states in responding to disasters, especially large ones. this cooperation may involve coordination of efforts, a loan or even the sharing of resources, but we must not forget some important aspects. the protection of people, infrastructure, property, the protection of the territory and of nature is an obligation of the states, a function which lies at the heart of their sovereignty. to be properly exercised, it requires robust own resources appropriate to the risks involved. civil protection services, by their very nature, require a strong connection to the territory and require a deep knowledge of the territory and the people. this also has implications for the chains of command, which even in situations of cooperation or aid must remain at the national level. the european union, which has not done all it could, for example in the field of supporting the member states in developing their own capacities, starting with prevention, now wants to do more than it should. the usurpation of chains of command for a supranational plan, for example, raises no small risks beyond a well-founded political objection.
mr president, i would like to thank the commissioner and also the rapporteur, mrs gardini, for the excellent work she has done, because this is what is actually being proposed for a civil protection mechanism with enhanced operational capacity and with specific references to the support given to the outermost regions in the event of natural disasters. it is crucial that this support is translated into a link between this mechanism, the structural funds and the solidarity fund. the commissioner here and some of my colleagues have said that the member states must in fact have control and responsibility for their territories. cooperation and solidarity must also begin within the territory itself, and it is therefore regrettable that the portuguese socialist government committed itself in november 2016 to reinforcing the cohesion fund for the recovery of madeira by eur 30 million after the fires in august, and that, to date, this reinforcement, this support, this solidarity have not yet been poured out and have not been more than empty words. prevention, risk management, the implementation of warning systems and the replacement of firefighting equipment may be at risk and therefore this solidarity must be poured out and must also have the support of the union.
mr president, the changes to the civil protection mechanism have created a precious reserve of material and human resources. it is good for european citizens and governments to realise that this mechanism will not replace the responsibilities of the member states themselves and that any intervention will always have to be pre-authorised. what does it change? investment in training and research, the creation of local intervention groups, the erasmus programme for civil protection, the strengthening of cooperation with third countries and a much more active role for the outermost regions. strengthening this mechanism means giving real meaning to the word solidarity in the most difficult times a member state can face. it also means increasing and giving greater protection to the citizens who are part of this union. there is nothing more important than the certainty that, in case of need, the duty to help will fall on all the members of this family, of this union. commissioner, i cannot but thank you for coming forward with this proposal at the right time. no one, no country, is immune to the unpredictability of a catastrophe.
mr president, i would naturally like to congratulate the commissioner and also to congratulate mrs gardini on the work they have been doing which has actually made it possible to create this first truly integrated system of civil protection. i must say that i have been advocating a european civil protection force for a long time and therefore i am not satisfied with the degree to which we have reached it. let us say that i think this is a very big step forward, given the situation we had before, but that we, the european union, must move towards an even more integrated and advanced system, with a real civil protection body for major disasters. so that means fires, floods, environmental accidents like nuclear or chemical accidents, earthquakes and other kinds of events. for that, i think we should look essentially at this point. no state, particularly small and medium-sized ones, has the resources to be able, on its own, to have all the means that are needed for large-scale disasters and so it is absolutely necessary that we have the capacity to share resources, because, as we know, it is very unlikely that a wave of disasters of this nature can be replicated at the same time on all sides and therefore this will allow savings for the european union, for european citizens, for european states and at the same time the advantages of having skills and knowledge and expertise in this area.
the 2018 report on turkey sets out the deep-rooted problems in the implementation of funds under the instrument for pre-accession assistance for the strengthening of civil society capacity and the promotion of social dialogue in turkey. it's not surprising. none of these objectives is in the interests of erdogan, who is too busy destroying democracy and the rule of law and violently repressing the kurdish minority, the opposition and anyone who dares to stand up to him. the commission and the council cannot allow themselves to be blackmailed by what they unfortunately say is ankara's good work on migration flows, as the report stresses, and they cannot turn a blind eye to afrin and the crushing of syrians and afghans by the turkish army on the border, allegedly through the use of cobra ii military vehicles financed by the european union. if this is confirmed, it's a disgrace, it's a disgrace to all of us. the commission and the council must respond to these very serious allegations. there are no conditions for continuing turkey's eu accession process, but the eu must, more than ever, continue to support the courageous turkish civil society. to do that, ipa or other support has to protect who it is supporting, but it also has to be scrutinized deeply. who is getting the money from european taxpayers today?
on behalf of the s&d group. madam president, the change in the institutional culture of the european union with regard to gender equality and the empowerment of women in its external relations is important, but it is also important that the commission commits itself to the preparation of a future communication on this subject. the report adopted in the committee on development and the committee on women includes important recommendations for the improvement of the second gender action plan with regard to sexual and reproductive rights as a condition for equality and empowerment of women, the possibility of resorting to abortion for victims of rape in conflict situations, the rights of lgbt people and the global gag rule, among others. we need to have concrete objectives and activities, linked to a strict timetable, as well as a qualitative evaluation of data demonstrating the real impact of the measures implemented in the beneficiary countries. otherwise, this gender action plan will be a mere tool for internal reporting. everything we are advocating here reaffirms the position of a decade of the european parliament. i therefore do not understand the epp's presentation of a minority resolution which eliminates many of these recommendations. the epp accuses the report of being too ideological in its action plan. but isn't conservatism in the plan of action also ideological? isn't that against equality? doesn't it become a tool against women's rights? doesn't it become another tool against lgbt rights? what is the epp's position on abortion in cases of rape? i do not think that all the epp members share this view, particularly because gender equality, as the epp rapporteur has rightly said, is one of the pillars on which this union is based. for us, standing up for equality and empowerment of women around the world is a matter of justice. tomorrow, our aim will be to maintain the text as approved in committee. i therefore ask members to vote against the minority proposal put forward by the european people's party.
madam president, the youth strategy cannot be reduced to a social welfare mechanism that is ineffective in combating precariousness. it is therefore necessary to demand more results. the first objective of this strategy must be the creation of effective employment for young people. this must be the main focus, fighting precariousness. the implementation of key european union programmes such as the youth employment initiative, the erasmus programmes or the european solidarity corps needs to be highlighted, while minimising asymmetries between european regions. we need more monitoring against abuse, where we have, for example, internships that are covering up employment contracts. we need european-wide whistleblowing mechanisms, with the collaboration of local authorities, to protect the anonymity of young people. more monitoring of the real effects of these programmes is needed. young people cannot be jumping from one internship to another without ever having an effective entry into the labour market. this strategy needs to include the most experienced workers, who play an important role in training young people in the workplace in a transition phase until their retirement, for effective generational renewal. commissioner, the objectives of this strategy are not in line with a 6% cut in the european social fund, as proposed by the commission. this situation will be even more acute in the case of the most disadvantaged member states and regions. we must fight this proposal.
the high rates of youth unemployment reflect a reality that cannot be dissociated from the eu policies that have promoted labour precariousness and deregulation, or the disinvestment in social services and benefits. the consequences of these policies are clearly seen in the increase in the number of young people who are now living in poverty and social exclusion. the responses created under this strategy, while palliative, could have a positive impact that is yet to be achieved. to make this possible, in addition to the necessary reinforcement of funds, skills and the scope of programmes, it would be imperative that they reject formulations of 'flexibility' which only translate the deregulation of working hours and work ties or any logic of creating unpaid traineeships, work in precarious conditions or the replacement of permanent jobs by temporary or traineeships. a youth policy geared to job creation and social inclusion is inseparable from the defence of employment with rights, based on collective bargaining, the rejection of any precarious link, the guarantee of fair and decent wages and regulated hours. but it is also inseparable from policies that promote free, universal and quality public services or public investment, which stimulate the economy and the productive and strategic sectors.
mr president, the response to citizens who are appealing to the european union in large numbers requires the utmost respect, which must be expressed in the quality of the response, which must be swift and cannot be evasive or out of step with regional, national and community political and administrative realities. we are dealing with citizens who trust the european union and who turn to us in the hope that we will solve their problems, even against the powers that be. commissioner, it is very serious that we receive numerous petitions from european citizens about precarious situations and the abuse of fixed-term employment contracts, in violation of a european directive, which is happening both in the private and public sectors. the numbers are frightening. in portugal, 82% of temporary workers are involuntarily employed, a situation which mainly affects young people, which, according to a commission assessment, shows a high degree of labour segregation which needs to be corrected. these are situations in which, contrary to their expectations, workers are forced to accept parttime, seasonal, open-ended or pseudo-independent work. as regards the abuse of successive fixed-term contracts, it is the governments of the member states themselves who are the first to violate the european directive, when they should be the first to set an example for the business sector. this is also the case in my country and in my region, especially in the teaching class. in the meantime, the commission ignores the pile of complaints and arranges bureaucratic subterfuge to delay or avoid responses that go against national governments. there is a duplicity of criteria which is unacceptable. if we are demanding from the private sector - mr president, global warming must be fought with clear and courageous decisions. the european union has set a binding and ambitious target of at least 40% reduction of greenhouse gas emissions in all sectors of the economy by 2030 and it is clear that the transport sector has a very important contribution to make to this target, and in particular heavy goods transport, given that a quarter of co2 emissions is due to this type of road transport. co2 emissions and fuel consumption of new heavy duty vehicles entering the european union are not, as we all know, subject to certification, monitoring or reporting, as is already the case with light passenger and commercial vehicles. and so this proposal for a regulation is welcome, because it fills a gap. only with robust and reliable data can you make good legislation. i have been attacked for this as a shadow rapporteur in the group currently working on the trilogues for the negotiations on the energy union governance regulation. an integrated approach from all sectors to achieve the desired objectives. this proposal lays down precise requirements for monitoring and reporting emissions, which will allow the establishment of a very positive, transparent database managed by the european environment agency and the annual report of the european commission will also be important. this regulation is part of the european strategy for low-carbon mobility and is an important step towards the decarbonisation of transport, but we cannot lose sight of the fact, as has already been said here, that in the shortest possible time we must have clean and intelligent models of mobility and that these clean and intelligent models of mobility must also be applied to heavy duty vehicles. this is another area where, in the interests of the health, sustainability and competitiveness of european industry, the european union must take a leading role on a global scale. thank you very much.

madam president, commissioner, i would first like to congratulate my colleague carlos iturgaiz on the consistent report he has produced and on the reflections he has put forward here. blue economy activities such as aquaculture are indeed a priority and their development should be strengthened, particularly in the outermost regions of the european union which are highly dependent on the sea and where most of the time they are not yet developed. in the azores, where i come from, with the support of the emff funds, aquaculture projects are being developed. these projects are involving fishermen and are intended to be a complementary activity to fishing, thus reducing the pressure on resources and finding alternatives for fishermen. the demand for proteins from the sea and the increase in agricultural production will have to take into account that the pressure on wild resources should not increase. aquaculture production cannot be sustained by blindly increasing the capture of wild resources such as small pelagic fish and krill, which serve as food for aquaculture production.
madam president, the development of aquaculture has considerable potential which has not yet been exploited. to do so sustainably requires strong support for scientific research and technological development, a requirement far removed from the everyday reality of state laboratories and other public institutions that are financially suffocated. reducing dependence on wild fish for the food of farmed species, improving the yield of the activity, increasing food conversion, cultivating native species, including freshwater species, algae and herbivorous species, along with reducing the environmental impacts of the activity, are lines to be pursued in the development of aquaculture. investment support is equally welcome and necessary. the development of aquaculture must be seen in a logic of complementarity with fisheries, which must safeguard small-scale, coastal and artisanal fisheries in particular and give priority to small and medium-sized enterprises. i am referring to complementarity, not substitution or alternative, or even competition, as some seem to erroneously suggest or point out.
madam president, first of all i would also like to congratulate our colleague, mrs norica nikolai, on her report. recreational fishing is an important activity throughout the european union, not only in terms of the number of people involved or the economic impact, but also in the context of resource management. the collection of data on creative fishing must be effective in order to assess the real impact of creative fishing. this is the only way to improve the scientific advice that supports multiannual plans and fisheries management in general. recreational fishing, as an activity of the blue economy, must be properly developed and closely monitored, particularly in the outermost regions such as the azores, where i come from. this activity has a significant social and economic impact in many ports and coastal areas, but there is room for improvement in its framework in places where there is a need to reorient fishermen to other activities. recreational fishing linked to tourism can be an excellent alternative and complementary, giving priority to traditional fishermen. the tourist fisheries sector must respect the tradition of small-scale fisheries.
madam president, recreational fishing, which is fishing for personal consumption or leisure, can take many forms and use many gear types, the most common being fishing by line, harpoon and catch, and there are also passive methods including nets, traps, nets and longlines. as it is becoming more widespread, it is necessary to know better the conditions under which this activity is carried out, to obtain more characterisation data, including on the number of fishermen and the most widely used gear, to assess the impact of the activity, the environmental impact on fisheries resources and the marine environment in general, the socio-economic impact in particular on small-scale, coastal and artisanal commercial fishing. it is also important to understand how far we are not dealing with situations of disguised commercial fishing. the protection of small-scale fisheries and their operators, the creation of conditions to ensure their viability and generational renewal must be a central concern and a priority.

madam president-in-office of the council, mr vice-president of the commission, i would first like to express my regret at what is happening in the mediterranean sea. what we have witnessed is absolutely unacceptable. you have not heard me condemn italy or malta because i believe that countries - in particular greece, italy, malta, spain - have been very much neglected by the european union in dealing with the migration issue and therefore this is a collective responsibility of the 28. we cannot let human beings die in the mediterranean sea, we must solve this issue once and for all. secondly, i would like to draw attention to the fact that it is essential for the council to give a signal of progress in the reform of the euro area in order to consolidate the euro project, in particular by definitively moving forward with banking union and by moving forward with another set of reforms aimed essentially at having a budget for the euro area which will make it possible to make this currency a friendly currency for all the economies that are part of the single currency. i would also like to say that it is essential to be quite firm on the brexit issue and, in particular, on the irish border issue. this is a key point which will be developed this week, today, in the british parliament. finally, since the vice-president has touched on the subject, let me speak about the financial perspective. it is unacceptable that there is a cohesion and convergence policy in which a country like lithuania loses 23% of the funds and a country like finland receives 5, a country like italy gets 6 and a country like croatia loses 6. this is not convergence, this is not cohesion, this is the opposite of convergence and cohesion, this cannot be accepted.
madam president, commissioner, the european union is showing its true face. migrants sinking in the mediterranean, boats at risk with pregnant women and children, come up against the cold inhumanity of those who choose to criminalize the humanitarian organizations involved in search and rescue operations. there is a lack of resources for cohesion, for supporting investment in the countries which need it most, for supporting employment with rights and for combating inequalities and asymmetries, but as the next european council will show, there is no lack of resources for the european military-industrial complex, for repressing migration and migrants, for further boosting the profits of the multinationals of the major european powers, and for the so-called structural reforms. another way of saying it is the privatization of the social functions of the state. the announced reform of the euro is just another way of tightening the shackles that impede the sovereign development of the peoples. defeating the eu to save europe is a demand with increased timeliness.
the euro has been a destructive element in portugal's economy. since portugal joined the euro, its public debt has risen from 55% to 130% of gdp. real wages have fallen by 20%, 500,000 young people have had to leave the country to look for work abroad and the question we ask is why does the european union insist on maintaining and deepening the economic and monetary union with the european monetary fund and with this proposal for pseudo-budgetary capacity of the euro zone? the answer is simple: the euro has always been and is a key political instrument for imposing structural reforms on member states by forcing countries like portugal to privatise their public services and strategic companies. it is obvious to us that the euro has not lived up to its promises of development and has disappointed expectations, and it is obvious to us that portugal must free itself from the constraints of the euro, regain its monetary sovereignty in order to be able to choose its own model of development.
mr president, the commission has come with a proposal on fiscal capacity for the eurozone which we really want discussed at the next euro summit. finally, the eurozone, which is the only monetary zone without a budget, recognised that we do indeed need a budget in order to have a future in the eurozone. however, what should be the objectives of this budget? there should be not only stability, but also growth and upward economic and social convergence. from this viewpoint, we need to go beyond the commission’s proposals, because if we want to support convergence, we should be clearly in favour of reforms, growth, increasing the sustainability of our social welfare systems, and coupling reforms with investments. something is missing in this instrument. secondly, the instrument to support investment is necessary not only to protect against shocks, but also to make sure that member states undertaking deep rebalances of the budgets can maintain a decent level of social investment, which is not the case so far. we need to go beyond, because if we do not improve these proposals, and we have combined these with co-financing being reduced for structural funds, we can already predict that divergences will continue in the eurozone.
mr president, commissioner, the recent turbulence on the markets caused by the italian political crisis has shown once again that it would be a mistake, and a serious mistake, to postpone an ambitious reform of economic and monetary union. and we all know what that reform of the euro should be. a european monetary fund worthy of the name, a new budgetary capacity, a full banking union and more democratic economic governance with the strengthening of the european parliament and respect for national parliaments. the june summit is therefore the moment of truth. so what we want to know, commissioner dombrovskis, is whether we can count on the commission to be on the right side until the last minute in defending an ambitious reform of the euro, which does not continue to put off until tomorrow what must be done today.
in writing. the completion of economic and monetary union (emu) is a priority if the european union in general and the euro area in particular are to be able to successfully meet the new challenges posed by changes in global geopolitics and the resulting economic balances, while responding to the expectations and needs of european citizens. in the course of the work to be done, the role of the european parliament as the direct representative of the peoples of europe must be valued and strengthened. in particular, it is essential that progress in the realisation of emu contributes to the stabilisation of the euro area, the promotion of convergence, the strengthening of investment and the creation of quality employment. the completion of the banking union with a strong guarantee system is a step that must be taken immediately at the same time as the european stabilisation mechanism must evolve consistently towards a european stabilisation fund that will benefit all member states in an integrated way and strengthen the overall competitiveness of the euro area.

madam president, in the 2014-2020 programming period, the circular economy was not recognised as a priority area of action in the european union. in the proposal for the next multiannual framework this objective has been included and i congratulate the commission on this decision. it is the recognition that inefficient use of resources leads to environmental, social and economic damage. in 2014, the european union produced 2.5 billion tonnes of waste. we need to reduce, recycle and reuse. it is therefore urgent to combat waste. if we want a more sustainable europe, the circular economy must be a commitment, not just in the long term, but as a permanent goal. the circular economy faces challenges that involve changing the entire production system and that need, in fact, the financial support of cohesion policy. we must therefore give local and regional authorities, small and mediumsized enterprises, investors, educational and research institutions adequate functional and financial autonomy, enabling them to prepare and implement the strategies they consider best for their regions, in particular community-based local development and integrated territorial investments. it is also essential to combine different funding sources, including research, and it is worth recalling the privileged use of the environmental fund, which, for example, will fully support the circular economy agenda of one of the outermost regions of europe, the autonomous region of madeira. in circular business, development and progress models are no longer based on profit alone, but on environmental protection. for this we must reinforce and rehabilitate the landfills, as has already been said, throughout the whole of europe. all direct and indirect investments in the circular economy are no longer an option, as the rapporteur says, they are a necessity for the good of our species and all life on this planet.

mr president, the 629 migrants who, instead of being rescued, were pushed adrift between italy and malta are but a thin expression of the hundreds of thousands of crossings in recent years. they are the faint remembrance of the 792 who died this year or the 16 000 who died in the last four years in the mediterranean, a common grave of oblivion. they are a clear demonstration of the total lack of solidarity of the eu and its powers towards those who have nothing to lose and risk their lives for better days. they can play the game of pushing between the eu and the member states. the responsibilities of those who close ports today are no different from those of those who did so before them, who criminalized rescue organizations, who signed criminal agreements with third countries to deter and contain migration. they are no different from the walls of spain, austria, hungary or greece, or from the real concentration camps on european soil. all this with the connivance of the eu and its xenophobic and racist policies which grossly violate human rights, international law.
the case of the aquarius, which the governments of italy and malta refused to take in, despite carrying 629 rescued people at sea, has served as a vivid reminder that the plight of migrants in the mediterranean is also the plight of europe, by calling into question the civilizational and humanistic principles that are its most valuable heritage and its main legacy. some progress in managing external borders, in helping refugees and in addressing the root causes of migration flows through development aid is not enough to hide the failure of the various attempts to share the burden of hosting refugees by distributing them in a solidarity-based and proportionate way among member states. it is clear that the italian and maltese governments have made a reprehensible and shocking choice in the case of the aquarius, turning their backs on human beings in danger, but it is also true that the effort required of these countries is unfair and that it is not enough to increase financial transfers to make it fair. it is regrettable that three years on, the eu has still not been able to reform the dublin regulation or impose consequences on member states that have taken refugees in.
in writing. in the face of the eu's cold inhumanity, some 800 people have already died this year while crossing the mediterranean. in the last four years, more than 16,000 have perished, making the mediterranean a giant mass grave. official figures that conceal a reality very likely even darker. driven by hunger, poverty and war - fleeing devastation scenarios in which the eu has heavy responsibilities - they bump into a wall of indifference and inhumanity, erected by the eu. the push-pull game we are seeing between the eu and the member states and between the member states is deplorable. how deplorable are attempts to criminalise the rescue and rescue organisations working on the ground. organisations whose action is necessary in the face of the omission and negligence of the eu and its governments in humanitarian rescue and salvage operations. and the agreements with third countries - particularly the one signed with turkey - to repel, contain or expel migrants are deplorable. eu agencies on the ground, rather than humanitarian action, focus on implementing these agreements. the 629 migrants adrift on the aquarius, including many children and pregnant women, are a telling expression of what the eu has become.

mr president, in a world marked by uncertainty and the fluidity of geopolitical relations, the negotiations of this eu-acp partnership agreement are an opportunity to set up a framework for cooperation based on respect for autonomy, human rights, the participation of civil society and sustainability, principles which are crucial for a humanistic and fair globalisation. it is also important to ensure that blocks maintain an overall position without prejudice to the specific approach in the negotiation plan. eu-acp cooperation must be a reference for the multiple partnerships the world needs, allocating resources that promote dignified and sustainable development, helping to reduce forced migration and the phenomena of extreme poverty and inequality that are eroding 21st century cities. and the resolution we are discussing here today is a commitment by the european parliament to this agenda for the future.

madam president, first of all i would like to express my full solidarity with the just struggle of interpreters. this european programme of industrial development in the field of defence is a good illustration of the nature of the dangers which the european union poses to the future of the peoples of the continent, particularly in view of the deepening of its militaristic dimension. this is a leap that cannot but unsettle all lovers of peace. the aim is to secure budgetary resources, diverted from programmes for civilian and peaceful purposes, to finance research and development of armaments and, at subsequent stages, also the production and purchase of such armaments. millions of euros are going to be poured into the military-industrial complex of the great european powers. the same people who never secured sufficient resources to achieve the economic and social cohesion so much advocated, to combat the effects of the crisis, poverty, social injustice, to support development, do not now hesitate to channel resources to boost the war industry. they are, at bottom, consistent in their action. those who threaten social progress and those who impose social regression on us are the same who threaten peace. we are here to express our firm opposition to this course.
the report is a first step towards a european union which is not only a european union of the people, but also a european union of the people. indigenous peoples, especially vulnerable and unprotected in the context of globalisation and climate change, continue to be victims of murder, aggression, forced eviction from their ancestral lands, arbitrary detention, persecution and intimidation, racism and exclusion. as if that were not enough, they have alarming levels of poverty, disease and illiteracy, insufficient political participation and representation and an accelerated loss of their cultural and linguistic heritage. but the scope of this report goes beyond the issue of the rights of indigenous peoples to cover the phenomenon of 'land grabbing', which affects not only these peoples but also numerous farming communities spread over various regions of the world. it is well known that the growing demand for natural resources by the extractive industry and agro-industrial sector is putting enormous pressure on territories traditionally inhabited and farmed by these communities, with conflicts over land use sometimes leading to serious violations of their human rights. one of the darkest aspects of this picture is the number of murders of indigenous and land rights defenders that have occurred in recent years. in 2017 alone, 312 activists were killed, two-thirds of them in defense of these rights. the european union must therefore give greater prominence to the situation of these communities in its external policy, in particular in its dialogue with third countries on human rights and in trade, cooperation and development agreements. the report also proposes a series of initiatives to improve the situation of these communities and protect their rights, starting with the adoption and implementation of the relevant international treaties by all the member states of the european union. in this chapter, ilo convention no 169 is never to be stressed again, as it is the only legally binding treaty. but a more robust approach is also proposed in the field of the european union's extraterritorial obligations and domestic activities with extraterritorial impact. this requires, first and foremost, that violations of the rights of these peoples, which directly or indirectly result from the extraterritorial actions of companies and investors under the jurisdiction of the european union, are properly investigated and sanctioned. it is in this spirit that this report proposes the creation of a european mechanism to enable these communities to lodge complaints about these violations, regardless of the country in which they are registered, so that they have effective access to justice. it is also recommended that development projects supported by the european union which have an impact on the livelihoods of these peoples should strictly respect their right to be informed and consulted in advance in order to obtain their prior consent. in parallel, the european union should carry out impact studies before concluding trade and cooperation agreements. finally, we cannot underestimate the importance of these communities for the conservation and sustainable use of biodiversity and for halting deforestation. that is also why the european union must include them in its strategies for sustainable development and the fight against climate change. this is a dimension which this report seeks to emphasize. indigenous peoples are often, as we all know, completely invisible people. people without a voice, seen as anachronisms, as people who are too much, who no longer have a place in our world. the european union must make an effort to make them more visible, and we hope that this report has made a small contribution to that end. before i close, i would like to stress the way in which the dialogue with all the groups in parliament on this subject has proceeded. this report has been greatly enriched by various contributions from representatives of various parliamentary groups in this parliament. i am convinced that the conditions are thereby created for a stronger report to come out of this and, above all, one that will be supported by a broad support from the members of this european parliament.
i would like to thank the rapporteur for his report, which is a very good report, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for his report. many of the food products we consume in the european union have indigenous blood in them. what is the occupation of land is done illegally, either by the owners of the agro-industry or by those who exploit natural resources in an absolutely illegal way, and this translates into the loss of life. the so-called democratic countries, such as those in the european union, cannot accept these criminal acts, nor the rape of indigenous women, nor the murder of indigenous women, as in the case of berta cáceres. i therefore attach importance to the contents of this report, which say that not everything is worthwhile. we have to protect the rights of indigenous peoples, their lands and their communities, but we also have to make sure that we don't negotiate trade agreements that are ultimately signed in ink that is actually the blood of lost lives.

madam president, commissioner, there is a very broad consensus today that the issue of climate change is one of the major problems facing humanity and that it can only be solved by a global response. this means that the various political actors, the various states and the various supranational bodies are obliged to develop intensive diplomatic activity to ensure that the responses to this problem are coordinated. the european union has been distinguished at world level by the way it has followed up this issue and sought to find solutions with a view to its resolution. it is obviously a complex issue, not one that can be resolved immediately, but above all it is one that can only be properly addressed if there is a policy articulation at international level, which means that our diplomatic action must always take this climate issue into account. in our relations with other countries, in our relations with other regions of the world, we have an obligation to put this issue at the centre of our diplomatic concerns, and i therefore wish to congratulate the authors of this report on the timeliness and quality of the report.

mr president, ladies and gentlemen, we have been talking informally about climate refugees for more than ten years, and i am therefore pleased to have been able to vote in favour of a report which is intended to finally give greater visibility to the phenomenon and to call for a definitive definition by the united nations. essentially unlike environmental migrants who move in search of better conditions, climate refugees find themselves without a home, without food, without basic living conditions, risking death from hunger, thirst and disease, all due to global phenomena caused by climate change. i am talking in particular of fires, floods, rising seas and oceans and extreme drought which are forcing people to flee, and which cannot be fully addressed within the framework of human rights and in the light of international law. instead of looking the other way and pretending that these situations don't exist and aren't typical, as has been done for the last ten years, it's time to take up our responsibility in climate diplomacy as europeans, leading this definition of refugees and creating the mechanisms necessary to protect these citizens.
madam president, the recent european council proved that something is wrong in the european union because we have in front of us a clear choice. on the one hand is the option of a europe that exploits citizens’ fears and advises a return to national borders, or of foot-dragging on important reforms, such as that of the eurozone for which we have been waiting for 10 years. on the other hand is the option of finally putting on the table at the european council the proposals which we know about and know should be adopted. the choice can no longer be delayed because if we delay until december, then forget it! the european council underlines the deep crisis in which the eu finds itself. its conclusions not only do not open up any prospects for overcoming it, but also contain new elements of crisis. the adoption of the 'country-specific recommendations' under the european semester renews the pressure to reduce primary public expenditure, in particular in the health sector, alongside the increase in debt repayment expenditure. the debate on deepening emu which was prompted by the conclusions of the franco-german summit makes it clear that, in the name of a supposed 'improvement' of the euro, the aim is to reinforce the chain of constraints and impositions which limit national sovereignty and thus the possibility of affirming sovereign development projects based on social progress. the militaristic and imperialist character of the eu, the 'european pillar' of nato, is being reinforced in accordance with the interests of its main powers, particularly france and germany. the concept of a 'fortress europe' is being consolidated, based on an even more selective, inhuman and exploitative view of migration, supported by measures of a security, militaristic and criminalising nature, which disregard the rights of migrants and refugees and foster xenophobic and racist sentiments. these findings "institutionalize" this view.
migration of peoples between regions and continents has always existed. genetic diversity, the fruitful interbreeding of traditions and cultures, the interpenetration of customs and beliefs are the watermark of human evolution. the issue of migration in the european union (eu) has been used by populist forces to implode the humanist and solidarity-based foundations of the partnership. the perception of the volume and impact of these flows is deliberately exaggerated and the positive consequences ignored. the 'agreement' reached in the european council is defensive and insufficient. a different approach is needed. turn the threat into an opportunity for change. a balanced distribution of migrants across the different eu countries requires convergent levels of prosperity and opportunities across all the union's territories. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the establishment of a european agency for the evaluation of medicinal products for human use (emah) is a step in the right direction. investing in improving the structural conditions of european economies will facilitate the integration of migrants, creating more and better opportunities for resident populations and creating conditions for the exploitation of the potential that can be extracted from a positive demographic shock.
mr president, the reality of the road transport sector is one of brutal exploitation of workers: extremely high levels of precariousness and very low wages, where illegal practices of payment per kilometre, tonne or journey are reported, and long working hours to meet the needs of the large economic groups. the right to be posted is limited and personal and family isolation is imposed during long periods of service, which some proposals make worse. the legitimate need for rest is neglected and the high levels of stress and physical and psychological exhaustion to which workers are subjected are ignored. the proposals under discussion are essentially aimed at deepening the mechanisms for exploiting workers, maximising profits and concentrating capital for the benefit of large companies and multinationals in the sector or sectors which depend on road transport, in particular for imports and exports. in the name of the single market, they promote a lower level of living and working conditions in europe, increasing inequalities, exclusion and poverty, options which, unfortunately, the portuguese government has joined.

member of the commission. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, thank you for a very lively debate, which is logical since this crisis has caused both pain and passion. but i want to say here, sides of the same coin and that they must go together. we have been able to play both sides in recent years, and certainly even more so in recent months. this strategy pays off in the end. so i want to avoid any triumphalism here, but i think that catastrophism would be very bad. today the truth is not that greece is out of the woods, because it is not out of the woods, but it is better, it is getting up and recovering step by step. by completing this programme, we are creating the conditions for a lasting and strong recovery. greece is returning to growth, and its fiscal performance is already impressive. unemployment is falling, of course too slowly, but it is falling nonetheless. however, there is still a lot of work to be done before the whole greek population can finally feel the positive effects of the efforts made in recent years. i know, coming back from greece, that the middle class and the most vulnerable still do not feel that the crisis is over and they are right, is balanced, responding both to the needs and interests of the greek people and to the concerns of the partners. it offers freedom - i emphasize that word again - and time for greece to be able to concentrate fully on the essentials - as many of you have said, in different terms, of course - namely, its economic recovery, the creation of strong growth. at the same time, it provides the necessary guarantees and strengthens the confidence of the economic partners of the investors to ensure a smooth return of greece to the financial markets. that is what this is about after 20 august. framework. of course it goes without saying that we fully respect the independence of the greek judiciary, but this is a matter that we need to follow up. reforms are needed, continuing the process of mr president, commissioner avramopoulos, ladies and gentlemen, back in 2015, in connection with the european agenda for security, i argued that our priority should be the three major existing union systems: the schengen information system the sis , eurodac and the vis the visa information system. the security of citizens, the free movement of europeans and the integrity of the schengen area are preserved by strengthening and making better use of these systems: an improved and reformed eurodac to reflect new challenges, mainly in relation to asylum, a strengthened and better used schengen information system to reflect new security challenges and, finally, a reformed visa information system, the vis, to reflect the challenges of mobility and migration. instead of new and multiple information systems, our investment and effort should be focused on existing systems, as was also clear from the schengen evaluations. the eurodac reform is about to be completed. the reform of the schengen information system, for which i had the honour of being your rapporteur, has just been completed. with the reform of the vis, for which i shall also have the honour of being the rapporteur for this parliament, we shall close this cycle of reforms. i am of course critical of the method used and the legislative hurry in this area, which is the result of the commission's excessive reactivity to requests from the member states. despite all this, the work carried out by the rapporteur, mrs kinga gál, was remarkable. the compromise reached reflects the majority position of this parliament. but let us be clear: the development and implementation of this system will raise numerous challenges, including meeting budgeted costs. the scrutiny of this system, as of the whole package of information systems, will have to be rigorous.

madam president, the president of the european commission has just presented us with a forward-looking agenda and an agenda presented by a true european politician. it is indeed true that the time for european sovereignty has come, and it has come at the forefront of the response to climate change, the digital revolution, trade, the need to manage migration differently. but why isn't this agenda moving forward? this agenda is not moving forward because we have forces from the past arguing that it is better to protect citizens only beyond national borders. this is a complete illusion because it is not possible today to protect citizens on the basis of national policies alone, solutions are needed at european level. but the real reason this agenda is not moving forward is that we have forces of the status quo, conservatives, who are resisting these european-scale solutions being truly implemented. for these forces of the status quo, in the matter of migration, it is enough to have an organized european border, but the progressive forces have to overcome this blockade because, in fact, it is not enough. we need a real partnership with africa, we need a european asylum system and we need to be up to the task when we take in refugees and we need to give them protection. the forces of the status quo think it's a good thing that we've had a resurgence in employment in europe and in investment, but the progressive forces want more because we need investment on a much larger scale to drive the energy transition and to drive the digital revolution in a good way. now, here we have a huge blockage that has to do with the reform of the euro zone. mr weber is pleased with where we are at but it is not possible because this euro zone continues to produce divergences until it has a functioning budgetary capacity. and then the conservative forces think it's enough to have jobs. it's not enough. the quality of jobs must be based on a genuine social pillar guaranteeing a contract of employment and social protection for all european citizens, wherever they are. the blockade can therefore only be overcome when the progressive forces succeed in overcoming the forces of the status quo which have been blocking genuine european solutions.

madam president, this debate has been an exercise in cynicism, propaganda and mystification. the logic of the commitment to defend the end of the unanimity rule, thus further strengthening the decision-making power of the major powers, was commended. the european sovereignty they speak of is the sovereignty of these powers, the power to establish and to dispose of and to impose their interests and the interests of their economic and financial groups. military expenditure associated with the defence fund has increased by more than twenty times, but mr juncker has the audacity to say that there is no militarization here. young people who are pushed into unemployment, underemployment or precariousness in life and work by the european union are given doses of reinforced propaganda. it is necessary to say that they are given the honour of being a mannequin to make up for their absence and thus a process of integration is under way which has run its course and has no solutions to the problems it creates, a process whose defeat is a condition for the construction of another europe, a europe of the workers and the peoples, a europe of peace and cooperation.
in writing. jean-claude juncker, in the context of a globally very appreciated intervention, stressed the need to continue along the path of concluding trade agreements with other regions of the world. that statement deserves my full agreement. what is already causing me deep concern is the fact that we are not moving resolutely towards the political and commercial agreement that we have been negotiating with mercosur for too many years. i believe that there will be no other group of countries which are closer to the eu culturally, politically, economically and socially. for a long time the responsibility for the delays in the negotiation process was laid on the shoulders of the latin american countries. but at the moment i think the biggest responsibilities lie with some european states and in some ways with the european commission itself. the european commissioner for agriculture has been acting more as an irish citizen and less as a european commissioner. the president of the commission should have seen that and acted accordingly. unfortunately, it has not yet done so, although i still expect it may do so. this trade agreement is of the greatest interest to both regions and there is no good reason why it should not be concluded in the shortest possible time.
president juncker's state of the union address presented an extensive letter of intent, which i generally agree with. i regret, however, that it comes only six months before the end of this parliamentary term. the strengthening of the coast and border guard and the revival of proposals for integrated border management are welcome. but i am alert to the need to do so in a way that is consistent with the necessary changes in the asylum and return system. i agree with the priorities for the end of the brexit negotiations, but i believe that safeguarding the rights of european citizens in the uk must be the first commitment. i support the ambition to strengthen the internationalisation of the euro, but i believe that we must deepen the reforms of the single currency in order to achieve economic and monetary union, promote more growth and employment and reduce public debt. i share the ambition to streamline the union's external policy by changing the decision-making process, particularly the majority system. i hope that the implementation of permanent structured cooperation will be achieved. i welcome the priority given to africa in the future of the union's external action.
in writing. the logic of compromise was praised in this debate, and the end of the unanimity rule was later advocated, thus further strengthening the decision-making power of the great powers. the 'european sovereignty' they speak of is the sovereignty of these powers, the power to set and dispose of, and to impose their interests and the interests of their economic and financial groups. military expenditure associated with the defence fund increases by more than 20 times, but mr juncker has the audacity to say that there is no militarization here. young people - who are being pushed into unemployment, underemployment and precariousness in work and life by the policies of the european union - are being fed propaganda in heightened doses. "they give you the honor of a mannequin, to make up for your absence". the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. the failure of this process is a condition for the construction of another europe, of the workers and peoples, of peace and cooperation.
the last year of the current european commission's term of office cannot be a transition or end-of-cycle year. it must be a year of action and strong motivation to strengthen and support all those who are striving for a more united, more ambitious and closer european union, and to weaken all those who promote anti-democratic populism to weaken the european project. this action involves creating the conditions for the adoption of a multiannual financial framework which promotes competitiveness, convergence and cohesion. it also means taking bold action to tackle the global challenges facing the european union such as trade war, security and defence, and protection against new technological risks. finally, it implies the ability to respond to the aspirations of european citizens by defining a policy of solidarity and transparency in the management of migration, by promoting the skills needed to create more and better jobs in the emerging society and by completing economic and monetary union in order to prevent new financial crises such as the one that followed the subprime crisis in the united states of america.
